The company builds an API-first, cloud-hosted electronic medical record (EMR) platform designed primarily for outpatient specialty clinics and tech-enabled healthcare organizations. Canvas positions itself as a modern, developer-friendly alternative to legacy EHR systems, with a strong emphasis on customizability through its Python SDK and FHIR API.\n     9→\n    10→Canvas has raised approximately $64.4M in total funding (Series B of $24M led by M13, with participation from Inspired Capital, IA Ventures, Upfront Ventures, Village Global, and others). The company achieved ONC certification in 2022. As of early 2026, Canvas serves 20+ named healthcare organizations across multiple outpatient specialties and was named the 2026 Best in KLAS Ambulatory Specialty EHR. The current CEO is Adam Farren (appointed September 2024); founder Andrew Hines serves as CTO. The company reports its customer base grew 300% following its self-serve platform launch.\n    11→\n    12→## Product: Canvas Medical\n    13→\n    14→CHPL IDs: 10904\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→Canvas Medical is a comprehensive, cloud-hosted EMR platform certified under ONC's Health IT Certification Program. The certified module (\"Canvas Medical 1\") covers a broad set of criteria spanning clinical documentation, CPOE, transitions of care, patient portal, public health reporting, and FHIR APIs — 35+ certified criteria in total. This is not a narrow specialty module; it is the full platform.\n    19→\n    20→The platform is API-first and built for extensibility. Canvas exposes its functionality through a FHIR R4 REST API (40 FHIR resources, 21 with write support) and a Python SDK that provides access to 47+ internal data models. The architecture is based on an open-source gRPC plugin runner where developers build \"handlers\" that respond to over 650 clinical and operational events and produce \"effects\" that modify data or the UI.\n    21→\n    22→Canvas offers pre-built EMR configurations for specific specialties (primary care, behavioral health, cardiology, weight loss/cardiometabolic, longevity, chronic care management, urgent care/telehealth, sleep medicine, gastroenterology, psychiatry) but the underlying platform is the same product. The specialization comes through configurable plugins and workflows, not separate codebases.\n    23→\n    24→### Users & Market\n    25→\n    26→Canvas targets two overlapping audiences:\n    27→\n    28→1. **Tech-enabled healthcare companies** (digital health startups, virtual-first clinics, value-based care organizations) that want a programmable EMR they can customize deeply via API and SDK. Named customers include Heartbeat Health, Wisp, Vida Health, Prax Health, Orchestra Health, Patina Health, Circulo, Vivante Health, and Isaac Health.\n    29→\n    30→2. **Traditional specialty practices** that want a modern, specialty-specific EMR with pre-built workflows and plugins — no developer required. Canvas offers a \"library of prebuilt plugins\" for these users.\n    31→\n    32→Day-to-day users include physicians, nurses, community health workers, billing staff, and practice managers. The CHPL SED intended user description is \"health care delivery users, not limited to clinicians,\" confirming it's used by non-clinical staff as well.\n    33→\n    34→Canvas operates exclusively in outpatient/ambulatory settings — no inpatient or hospital functionality is described. Clinical settings include solo practices, multi-site groups, virtual care organizations, and chronic care management programs. Canvas won the 2026 Best in KLAS award specifically in the \"Ambulatory Specialty EHR\" category, and KLAS users highlighted its adaptability to non-fee-for-service and value-based care models.\n    35→\n    36→### Modules & Functionality\n    37→\n    38→Based on vendor materials, developer documentation, and press coverage, Canvas Medical includes the following functionality:\n    39→\n    40→**Clinical Documentation & Charting**\n    41→- Proprietary \"Narrative Charting\" system (claimed 3x faster with 80% fewer clicks than menu-based EMRs)\n    42→- \"Commands\" — structured chart documentation units that form the building blocks of clinical notes\n    43→- Clinical notes/encounters linked to assessments, reason for visit, billing codes\n    44→- Protocol engine for clinical decision support (active protocols, protocol overrides/snoozing)\n    45→- Banner alerts for patient-level notifications\n    46→\n    47→**Orders & Prescribing**\n    48→- CPOE for medications, laboratory, and diagnostic imaging (per ONC certification)\n    49→- Drug-drug and drug-allergy interaction checking\n    50→- E-prescribing via Surescripts integration (including controlled substances implied by certification criteria)\n    51→- Lab ordering and results via Health Gorilla integration\n    52→- Referral management\n    53→\n    54→**Patient Records**\n    55→- Demographics, family health history, implantable device tracking\n    56→- Allergy/intolerance management\n    57→- Problem list / conditions\n    58→- Medication list (active medications, medication history, medication statements, compound medications, stop medication events)\n    59→- Immunization records\n    60→- Vitals and observations\n    61→- Care plans and care teams\n    62→- Patient consents\n    63→- Goals\n    64→\n    65→**Scheduling**\n    66→- Appointment scheduling with calendar management\n    67→- Provider schedules and time slots\n    68→- Facility and practice location management\n    69→\n    70→**Billing & Revenue Cycle**\n    71→- Integrated billing with claims submission via Claim.MD integration\n    72→- Billing line items linked to clinical notes\n    73→- Charge Description Master (CDM)\n    74→- Payor-specific charges\n    75→- Coverage/insurance management\n    76→- Eligibility checking and summaries (copay, coinsurance)\n    77→- Payment posting and claim payment tracking\n    78→- Support for fee-for-service, value-based, and direct-to-consumer membership payment models\n    79→- HCC (Hierarchical Condition Category) coding support\n    80→- AI-powered \"Claim Coding Agent\" for automated code suggestions\n    81→\n    82→**Patient Engagement & Communication**\n    83→- Patient portal (view, download, transmit per (e)(1) certification)\n    84→- Secure messaging (Message data model for sent/received messages)\n    85→- Patient letters/correspondence\n    86→- Open-source patient app\n    87→- Questionnaires and questionnaire responses\n    88→\n    89→**AI & Automation**\n    90→- Hyperscribe: open-source AI clinical documentation copilot (ambient scribe)\n    91→- Claim Coding Agent: automated revenue cycle coding\n    92→- Parsing Agent: document and lab data processing\n    93→- Plugin-based workflow automation (650+ triggerable events)\n    94→- Natural language plugin creation via Claude Code integration\n    95→\n    96→**Interoperability & Data Exchange**\n    97→- FHIR R4 API with 40 resources\n    98→- C-CDA generation and import\n    99→- Transitions of care (send/receive)\n   100→- Immunization registry reporting ((f)(1))\n   101→- Electronic case reporting ((f)(5))\n   102→- SMART on FHIR app embedding\n   103→- Webhooks\n   104→- External event ingestion (ADT feeds)\n   105→- Pre-built integrations: Surescripts (e-prescribing), Health Gorilla (labs), Claim.MD (claims)\n   106→- Third-party integration clients for AI/ML, cloud storage, email, messaging\n   107→\n lines (patient groups sharing brands)\n   114→- Audit logging (per (d) criteria certification)\n   115→\n   116→### Data & Content\n   117→\n   118→The Canvas Medical SDK documentation provides an unusually transparent view of the platform's internal data model, listing 47+ data objects. Based on this and the FHIR API documentation, the platform stores:\n   119→\n   120→**Clinical data**: Patient demographics, conditions/diagnoses, allergies/intolerances, medications (active, historical, statements, compounds, stop events), immunizations, lab results, imaging results, observations/vitals, procedures, devices/implantables, clinical notes/encounters, assessments, care plans, care teams, goals, clinical protocols, detected issues, diagnostic reports, specimens.\n   121→\n   122→**Orders & prescriptions**: Medication requests, medication dispense records, lab orders, imaging orders, referrals.\n   123→\n   124→**Administrative data**: Appointments, schedules, calendars, patient consents, tasks, staff records, user accounts, organizations, facilities, practice locations, teams, business lines, value sets, service providers.\n   125→\n   126→**Financial data**: Claims, billing line items, charge description master entries, payor-specific charges, coverage/insurance, eligibility summaries, payment postings.\n   127→\n   128→**Communication data**: Messages (sent/received), letters, questionnaires and responses, banner alerts, external events (ADT).\n   129→\n   130→**Documents**: C-CDA documents, uncategorized clinical documents, media/attachments, document references.\n   131→\n   132→**Audit & system data**: Provenance records, application/plugin definitions, common enumeration types.\n   133→\n   134→The breadth of the SDK data model (47 objects covering clinical, financial, administrative, and communication domains) combined with the FHIR API (40 resources) strongly suggests this platform stores a comprehensive set of ambulatory healthcare data — essentially everything needed to run an outpatient practice including clinical care, billing, scheduling, and patient communication.\n   135→\n   136→---\n   137→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. The company builds an API-first, cloud-hosted electronic medical record (EMR) platform designed primarily for outpatient specialty clinics and tech-enabled healthcare organizations. Canvas positions itself as a modern, developer-friendly alternative to legacy EHR systems, with a strong emphasis on customizability through its Python SDK and FHIR API.\n\nCanvas has raised approximately $64.4M in total funding (Series B of $24M led by M13, with participation from Inspired Capital, IA Ventures, Upfront Ventures, Village Global, and others). The company achieved ONC certification in 2022. As of early 2026, Canvas serves 20+ named healthcare organizations across multiple outpatient specialties and was named the 2026 Best in KLAS Ambulatory Specialty EHR. The current CEO is Adam Farren (appointed September 2024); founder Andrew Hines serves as CTO. The company reports its customer base grew 300% following its self-serve platform launch.\n\n## Product: Canvas Medical\n\nCHPL IDs: 10904\n\n### What It Is\n\nCanvas Medical is a comprehensive, cloud-hosted EMR platform certified under ONC's Health IT Certification Program. The certified module (\"Canvas Medical 1\") covers a broad set of criteria spanning clinical documentation, CPOE, transitions of care, patient portal, public health reporting, and FHIR APIs — 35+ certified criteria in total. This is not a narrow specialty module; it is the full platform.\n\nThe platform is API-first and built for extensibility. Canvas exposes its functionality through a FHIR R4 REST API (40 FHIR resources, 21 with write support) and a Python SDK that provides access to 47+ internal data models. The architecture is based on an open-source gRPC plugin runner where developers build \"handlers\" that respond to over 650 clinical and operational events and produce \"effects\" that modify data or the UI.\n\nCanvas offers pre-built EMR configurations for specific specialties (primary care, behavioral health, cardiology, weight loss/cardiometabolic, longevity, chronic care management, urgent care/telehealth, sleep medicine, gastroenterology, psychiatry) but the underlying platform is the same product. The specialization comes through configurable plugins and workflows, not separate codebases.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nCanvas targets two overlapping audiences:\n\n1. **Tech-enabled healthcare companies** (digital health startups, virtual-first clinics, value-based care organizations) that want a programmable EMR they can customize deeply via API and SDK. Named customers include Heartbeat Health, Wisp, Vida Health, Prax Health, Orchestra Health, Patina Health, Circulo, Vivante Health, and Isaac Health.\n\n2. **Traditional specialty practices** that want a modern, specialty-specific EMR with pre-built workflows and plugins — no developer required. Canvas offers a \"library of prebuilt plugins\" for these users.\n\nDay-to-day users include physicians, nurses, community health workers, billing staff, and practice managers. The CHPL SED intended user description is \"health care delivery users, not limited to clinicians,\" confirming it's used by non-clinical staff as well.\n\nCanvas operates exclusively in outpatient/ambulatory settings — no inpatient or hospital functionality is described. Clinical settings include solo practices, multi-site groups, virtual care organizations, and chronic care management programs. Canvas won the 2026 Best in KLAS award specifically in the \"Ambulatory Specialty EHR\" category, and KLAS users highlighted its adaptability to non-fee-for-service and value-based care models.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials, developer documentation, and press coverage, Canvas Medical includes the following functionality:\n\n**Clinical Documentation & Charting**\n- Proprietary \"Narrative Charting\" system (claimed 3x faster with 80% fewer clicks than menu-based EMRs)\n- \"Commands\" — structured chart documentation units that form the building blocks of clinical notes\n- Clinical notes/encounters linked to assessments, reason for visit, billing codes\n- Protocol engine for clinical decision support (active protocols, protocol overrides/snoozing)\n- Banner alerts for patient-level notifications\n\n**Orders & Prescribing**\n- CPOE for medications, laboratory, and diagnostic imaging (per ONC certification)\n- Drug-drug and drug-allergy interaction checking\n- E-prescribing via Surescripts integration (including controlled substances implied by certification criteria)\n- Lab ordering and results via Health Gorilla integration\n- Referral management\n\n**Patient Records**\n- Demographics, family health history, implantable device tracking\n- Allergy/intolerance management\n- Problem list / conditions\n- Medication list (active medications, medication history, medication statements, compound medications, stop medication events)\n- Immunization records\n- Vitals and observations\n- Care plans and care teams\n- Patient consents\n- Goals\n\n**Scheduling**\n- Appointment scheduling with calendar management\n- Provider schedules and time slots\n- Facility and practice location management\n\n**Billing & Revenue Cycle**\n- Integrated billing with claims submission via Claim.MD integration\n- Billing line items linked to clinical notes\n- Charge Description Master (CDM)\n- Payor-specific charges\n- Coverage/insurance management\n- Eligibility checking and summaries (copay, coinsurance)\n- Payment posting and claim payment tracking\n- Support for fee-for-service, value-based, and direct-to-consumer membership payment models\n- HCC (Hierarchical Condition Category) coding support\n- AI-powered \"Claim Coding Agent\" for automated code suggestions\n\n**Patient Engagement & Communication**\n- Patient portal (view, download, transmit per (e)(1) certification)\n- Secure messaging (Message data model for sent/received messages)\n- Patient letters/correspondence\n- Open-source patient app\n- Questionnaires and questionnaire responses\n\n**AI & Automation**\n- Hyperscribe: open-source AI clinical documentation copilot (ambient scribe)\n- Claim Coding Agent: automated revenue cycle coding\n- Parsing Agent: document and lab data processing\n- Plugin-based workflow automation (650+ triggerable events)\n- Natural language plugin creation via Claude Code integration\n\n**Interoperability & Data Exchange**\n- FHIR R4 API with 40 resources\n- C-CDA generation and import\n- Transitions of care (send/receive)\n- Immunization registry reporting ((f)(1))\n- Electronic case reporting ((f)(5))\n- SMART on FHIR app embedding\n- Webhooks\n- External event ingestion (ADT feeds)\n- Pre-built integrations: Surescripts (e-prescribing), Health Gorilla (labs), Claim.MD (claims)\n- Third-party integration clients for AI/ML, cloud storage, email, messaging\n\n**Administrative**\n- Staff and user management\n- Organization and facility configuration\n- Team and care team management\n- Task management system\n- Business lines (patient groups sharing brands)\n- Audit logging (per (d) criteria certification)\n\n### Data & Content\n\nThe Canvas Medical SDK documentation provides an unusually transparent view of the platform's internal data model, listing 47+ data objects. Based on this and the FHIR API documentation, the platform stores:\n\n**Clinical data**: Patient demographics, conditions/diagnoses, allergies/intolerances, medications (active, historical, statements, compounds, stop events), immunizations, lab results, imaging results, observations/vitals, procedures, devices/implantables, clinical notes/encounters, assessments, care plans, care teams, goals, clinical protocols, detected issues, diagnostic reports, specimens.\n\n**Orders & prescriptions**: Medication requests, medication dispense records, lab orders, imaging orders, referrals.\n\n**Administrative data**: Appointments, schedules, calendars, patient consents, tasks, staff records, user accounts, organizations, facilities, practice locations, teams, business lines, value sets, service providers.\n\n**Financial data**: Claims, billing line items, charge description master entries, payor-specific charges, coverage/insurance, eligibility summaries, payment postings.\n\n**Communication data**: Messages (sent/received), letters, questionnaires and responses, banner alerts, external events (ADT).\n\n**Documents**: C-CDA documents, uncategorized clinical documents, media/attachments, document references.\n\n**Audit & system data**: Provenance records, application/plugin definitions, common enumeration types.\n\nThe breadth of the SDK data model (47 objects covering clinical, financial, administrative, and communication domains) combined with the FHIR API (40 resources) strongly suggests this platform stores a comprehensive set of ambulatory healthcare data — essentially everything needed to run an outpatient practice including clinical care, billing, scheduling, and patient communication.\n\n---\n","numLines":137,"startLine":1,"totalLines":137}}}

pagelayout pagelayout__centerdocs\" id=\"search_container\"><div class=\"search-sidenav\"><div id=\"refinementlist\"></div></div><div class=\"search-panel__results\"><div id=\"algolia-results\" class=\"navigation__search\"></div><div id=\"pagination\"></div></div></div></div><div id=\"search-overlay\"></div> <main><div class=\"pagelayout pagelayout__centerdocs\"><div class=\"sidenav sidenavtoc\"> <nav class=\"toc\" role=\"navigation\"><div class=\"toc__links\"></div> </nav></div><div class=\"pagelayout__centerdocs__wrapper\"><div class=\"pagelayout__centerdocs__content\"> <article class=\"article\" itemscope itemtype=\"http://schema.org/BlogPosting\"><div class=\"article__container\"><div class=\"article__container__inner\"> <header class=\"article__header\"><h1 class=\"article__title\" itemprop=\"name headline\">EHI Export</h1><p class=\"article__meta\"> <time datetime=\"2025-11-13T09:27:04-08:00\" itemprop=\"datePublished\"> Last updated: 13 Nov 2025 </time></p> </header><div class=\"article__content\" itemprop=\"articleBody\"><p>Canvas supports the <b>export of electronic health information (EHI)</b> based on FHIR Bulk Data Access.</p><h2 id=\"creating-a-single-patient-export\" class='article__heading'> Creating a Single Patient Export <a href=\"#creating-a-single-patient-export\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h2><h3 id=\"in-the-ui\" class='article__heading'> In the UI <a href=\"#in-the-ui\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h3><p>To generate a single patient export, navigate to <code class=\"language-plaintext highlighter-rouge\">Fhir bulk data exports</code> in your admin settings.</p><p>Click on <code class=\"language-plaintext highlighter-rouge\">ADD FHIR BULK DATA EXPORT</code> to pull up the tool. Select the appropriate Oauth application from the dropdown. These are typically created by your team as a part of implementation. Support can also help to create one if needed. Export type defaults to ‘Patient’. Search for the patient and click <code class=\"language-plaintext highlighter-rouge\">Start Export</code>.</p><p>The export will generate links to the output files for each resource. Clicking each will download the files locally.</p><aside class=\"alert alert__wrapper alert__warning\" role=\"alert\"><div class=\"alert__icon\"> <svg xmlns=\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\" viewBox=\"0 0 512 512\"> <path d=\"M256 512a256 256 0 1 0 0-512 256 256 0 1 0 0 512zm0-384c13.3 0 24 10.7 24 24v112c0 13.3-10.7 24-24 24s-24-10.7-24-24V152c0-13.3 10.7-24 24-24zm-32 224a32 32 0 1 1 64 0 32 32 0 1 1-64 0z\"/> </svg></div><div class=\"alert__content\"> This functionality is permission based. If you do not have access and need it, please reach out to your internal administrators. They can work with our support team to ensure the permissions are assigned to the necessary groups.</div> </aside><h3 id=\"using-the-api\" class='article__heading'> Using the API <a href=\"#using-the-api\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h3><p>You can initiate a single patient export by issuing a request like the one below:<br/> <code class=\"language-plaintext highlighter-rouge\">GET https://fumage-&lt;instance-name&gt;.canvasmedical.com/Patient/&lt;id&gt;/$export</code></p><p><br/></p><h2 id=\"creating-a-population-export\" class='article__heading'> Creating a Population Export <a href=\"#creating-a-population-export\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h2><p>Canvas also supports exporting EHI for a group of patients or all patients. To request an export, please contact <a href=\"https://portal.usepylon.com/canvas-medical/forms/standard\" target=\"_blank\">support</a> and provide the following information:</p><ul><li>Your instance name (the url of your instance)</li><li>Your username</li><li>Your role</li><li>The group of patients, or specify all patients</li><li>The purpose of the export.</li></ul><aside class=\"alert alert__wrapper alert__info\" role=\"alert\"><div class=\"alert__icon\"> <svg xmlns=\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\" viewBox=\"0 0 512 512\"> <path d=\"M256 512a256 256 0 1 0 0-512 256 256 0 1 0 0 512zm-40-176h24v-64h-24c-13.3 0-24-10.7-24-24s10.7-24 24-24h48c13.3 0 24 10.7 24 24v88h8c13.3 0 24 10.7 24 24s-10.7 24-24 24h-80c-13.3 0-24-10.7-24-24s10.7-24 24-24zm40-208a32 32 0 1 1 0 64 32 32 0 1 1 0-64z\"/> </svg></div><div class=\"alert__content\"> Please note that population exports may take a significant time to process depending on the amount of data in your instance.</div> </aside><p>The Canvas team can initiate the bulk export on your behalf. The output will be availble within the <code class=\"language-plaintext highlighter-rouge\">ADD FHIR BULK DATA EXPORT</code> table.</p><h2 id=\"format\" class='article__heading'> Format <a href=\"#format\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h2><p>EHI is exported as standard FHIR R4 resources in Newline Delimited JSON format, as outlined by the <a href=\"https://hl7.org/fhir/uv/bulkdata/STU1.0.1/\">FHIR Bulk Data Access pattern</a>. <br/> <br/></p><h3 id=\"resources\" class='article__heading'> Resources <a href=\"#resources\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h3><p>The resources in scope for the response are:</p><ul><li>The patient resource itself.</li><li>Any resource that is associated with the patient.</li></ul><p>The table below lists the Canvas resources included in the patient recordset:</p><table><thead><tr><th>Resource</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><a href=\"/api/allergyintolerance\">AllergyIntolerance</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/appointment\">Appointment</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/careplan\">CarePlan</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/careteam\">CareTeam</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/claim\">Claim</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/communication\">Communication</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/condition\">Condition</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/consent\">Consent</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/coverage\">Coverage</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/coverageeligibilityresponse\">CoverageEligibilityResponse</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/device\">Device</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/diagnosticreport\">DiagnosticReport</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/documentreference\">DocumentReference</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/encounter\">Encounter</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/goal\">Goal</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/immunization\">Immunization</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/media\">Media</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/medicationdispense\">MedicationDispense</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/medicationrequest\">MedicationRequest</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/relatedperson\">RelatedPerson</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/servicerequest\">ServiceRequest</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/specimen\">Specimen</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/task\">Task</a></td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div> </article></div><div class=\"sidepanels\"><div class=\"sidepanels__sidepanel\"></div><div class=\"sidepanels__sidepanel\"><div API</a></li></ul></li><li class=\"anchorlist__item\"><a href=\"#creating-a-population-export\">Creating a Population Export</a></li><li class=\"anchorlist__item\"><a href=\"#format\">Format</a><ul <article class=\"article\" itemscope itemtype=\"http://schema.org/BlogPosting\"><div class=\"article__container\"><div class=\"article__container__inner\"> <header class=\"article__header\"><h1 class=\"article__title\" itemprop=\"name headline\">EHI Export</h1><p class=\"article__meta\"> <time datetime=\"2025-11-13T09:27:04-08:00\" itemprop=\"datePublished\"> Last updated: 13 Nov 2025 </time></p> </header><div class=\"article__content\" itemprop=\"articleBody\"><p>Canvas supports the <b>export of electronic health information (EHI)</b> based on FHIR Bulk Data Access.</p><h2 id=\"creating-a-single-patient-export\" class='article__heading'> Creating a Single Patient Export <a href=\"#creating-a-single-patient-export\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h2><h3 id=\"in-the-ui\" class='article__heading'> In the UI <a href=\"#in-the-ui\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h3><p>To generate a single patient export, navigate to <code class=\"language-plaintext highlighter-rouge\">Fhir bulk data exports</code> in your admin settings.</p><p>Click on <code class=\"language-plaintext highlighter-rouge\">ADD FHIR BULK DATA EXPORT</code> to pull up the tool. Select the appropriate Oauth application from the dropdown. These are typically created by your team as a part of implementation. Support can also help to create one if needed. Export type defaults to ‘Patient’. Search for the patient and click <code class=\"language-plaintext highlighter-rouge\">Start Export</code>.</p><p>The export will generate links to the output files for each resource. Clicking each will download the files locally.</p><aside class=\"alert alert__wrapper alert__warning\" role=\"alert\"><div class=\"alert__icon\"> <svg xmlns=\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\" viewBox=\"0 0 512 512\"> <path d=\"M256 512a256 256 0 1 0 0-512 256 256 0 1 0 0 512zm0-384c13.3 0 24 10.7 24 24v112c0 13.3-10.7 24-24 24s-24-10.7-24-24V152c0-13.3 10.7-24 24-24zm-32 224a32 32 0 1 1 64 0 32 32 0 1 1-64 0z\"/> </svg></div><div class=\"alert__content\"> This functionality is permission based. If you do not have access and need it, please reach out to your internal administrators. They can work with our support team to ensure the permissions are assigned to the necessary groups.</div> </aside><h3 id=\"using-the-api\" class='article__heading'> Using the API <a href=\"#using-the-api\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h3><p>You can initiate a single patient export by issuing a request like the one below:<br/> <code class=\"language-plaintext highlighter-rouge\">GET https://fumage-&lt;instance-name&gt;.canvasmedical.com/Patient/&lt;id&gt;/$export</code></p><p><br/></p><h2 id=\"creating-a-population-export\" class='article__heading'> Creating a Population Export <a href=\"#creating-a-population-export\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h2><p>Canvas also supports exporting EHI for a group of patients or all patients. To request an export, please contact <a href=\"https://portal.usepylon.com/canvas-medical/forms/standard\" target=\"_blank\">support</a> and provide the following information:</p><ul><li>Your instance name (the url of your instance)</li><li>Your username</li><li>Your role</li><li>The group of patients, or specify all patients</li><li>The purpose of the export.</li></ul><aside class=\"alert alert__wrapper alert__info\" role=\"alert\"><div class=\"alert__icon\"> <svg xmlns=\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\" viewBox=\"0 0 512 512\"> <path d=\"M256 512a256 256 0 1 0 0-512 256 256 0 1 0 0 512zm-40-176h24v-64h-24c-13.3 0-24-10.7-24-24s10.7-24 24-24h48c13.3 0 24 10.7 24 24v88h8c13.3 0 24 10.7 24 24s-10.7 24-24 24h-80c-13.3 0-24-10.7-24-24s10.7-24 24-24zm40-208a32 32 0 1 1 0 64 32 32 0 1 1 0-64z\"/> </svg></div><div class=\"alert__content\"> Please note that population exports may take a significant time to process depending on the amount of data in your instance.</div> </aside><p>The Canvas team can initiate the bulk export on your behalf. The output will be availble within the <code class=\"language-plaintext highlighter-rouge\">ADD FHIR BULK DATA EXPORT</code> table.</p><h2 id=\"format\" class='article__heading'> Format <a href=\"#format\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h2><p>EHI is exported as standard FHIR R4 resources in Newline Delimited JSON format, as outlined by the <a href=\"https://hl7.org/fhir/uv/bulkdata/STU1.0.1/\">FHIR Bulk Data Access pattern</a>. <br/> <br/></p><h3 id=\"resources\" class='article__heading'> Resources <a href=\"#resources\" class=\"article__anchor\">#</a></h3><p>The resources in scope for the response are:</p><ul><li>The patient resource itself.</li><li>Any resource that is associated with the patient.</li></ul><p>The table below lists the Canvas resources included in the patient recordset:</p><table><thead><tr><th>Resource</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><a href=\"/api/allergyintolerance\">AllergyIntolerance</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/appointment\">Appointment</a></td></tr><tr><td><a href=\"/api/careplan\">CarePlan</a></td></tr><tr><td><a error: string }[];\n  resources: FhirResource[];\n}\n\nfunction stripHtml(html: string): string {\n  return html\n    .replace(/<[^>]+>/g, \" \")\n    .replace(/&amp;/g, \"&\")\n    .replace(/&lt;/g, \"<\")\n    .replace(/&gt;/g, \">\")\n    .replace(/&quot;/g, '\"')\n    .replace(/&#39;/g, \"'\")\n    .replace(/&nbsp;/g, \" \")\n    .replace(/\\s+/g, \" \")\n    .trim();\n}\n\nfunction extractFieldsFromApidocObjects(html: string): FhirField[] {\n  const fields: FhirField[] = [];\n\n  // Match top-level apidoc__object blocks. class=\"apidoc__object\">(.*?)(?=<div class=\"apidoc__object\">|<div class=\"apidoc__object__children__panel\">|$)/gs;\n\n  // Simpler approach: extract name+type+description sequences\n  const OUTPUT = join(import.meta.dir, \"fhir-api-resources.json\");\n\ninterface FhirField {\n  name: string;\n  type: string;\n  required: boolean;\n  description: string;\n  valueOptions?: .replace(/<[^>]+>/g, \" \")\n    .replace(/&amp;/g, \"&\")\n    .replace(/&lt;/g, \"<\")\n    .replace(/&gt;/g, \">\")\n    .replace(/&quot;/g, '\"')\n    .replace(/&#39;/g, \"'\")\n    .replace(/&nbsp;/g, \" \")\n    .replace(/\\s+/g, \" \")\n    .trim();\n}\n\nfunction parseApiPage(\n  filename: string,\n  content: string\n): FhirResource | null {\n  // Skip non-resource pages\n  \"pagination\",\n    \"date-filtering\",\n    \"errors\",\n    \"software-requirements\",\n  ];\n  if (skipPages.includes(filename.replace(\".html\", \"\"))) {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  // Extract startIndex: number;\n  }[] = [];\n\n  while ((m = opInfoPattern.exec(content)) !== null) {\n    opPositions.push({\n      id: m[1],\n      method: stripHtml(m[3]).toUpperCase(),\n      path: next operation\n  for (let i = 0; i < opPositions.length; i++) {\n    const op = opPositions[i];\n    const nextStart =\n      i + 1 < opPositions.length\n        ? opPositions[i + 1].startIndex\n        : content.length;\n    const opSection = content.substring(op.startIndex, nextStart);\n\n    // Extract fields from apidoc__object blocks in this section\n    const fields: FhirField[] = [];\n    const fieldPattern =\n      /<div class=\"apidoc__object\">\\s*<div class=\"apidoc__object__name\">\\s*(.*?)\\s*<\\/div>/gs;\n\n    // Use a more comprehensive approach: find all objects with their attributes\n    // by scanning for the apidoc__object blocks\n    const objectBlocks = opSection.split(\n      /<div class=\"apidoc__object\">/\n    );\n\n    for (let j = 1; j < objectBlocks.length; nameMatch ? stripHtml(nameMatch[1]) : \"\";\n\n      // Type\n      const typeMatch = block.match(\n        /<div class=\"apidoc__object__type\">\\s*(.*?)\\s*<\\/div>/s\n      );\n      const type = ];\n      const desc = descMatches\n        .map((dm) => stripHtml(dm[1]))\n        .filter((d) => d.length > 0)\n        .join(\" \");\n\n      // Value options from enum_options\n      const enumMatch = block.match(\n        /<div class=\"apidoc__object__enum_options\">\\s*(.*?)\\s*<\\/div>/s\n      );\n      let valueOptions: string[] | undefined;\n      if (enumMatch) {\n        const enumText = stripHtml(enumMatch[1]);\n        if (enumText.length > 0) {\n          valueOptions = enumText\n            .split(/\\s+/)\n            .filter((v) => v.length > 0);\n        }\n      }\n\n      if (name) {\n        fields.push({\n          name,\n          type,\n          required,\n          description: desc,\n          ...(valueOptions && valueOptions.length > 0\n            ? { valueOptions }\n            : {}),\n        });\n      }\n    }\n\n    operations.push({\n      method: op.method,\n      path: op.path,\n      summary: op.summary,\n      description: op.description,\n      attributes: fields,\n    });\n  }\n\n  const totalFields = operations.reduce(\n    (sum, op) => sum + op.attributes.length,\n    0\n  );\n\n  return {\n    name: resourceName,\n totalFields,\n  };\n}\n\n// Main\nconst files = readdirSync(API_DIR).filter((f) => f.endsWith(\".html\"));\nconst result: ExtractionResult = {\n  extractionDate: new Date().toISOString(),\n  totalFiles: files.length,\n  totalParsed: 0,\n  parseFailures: [],\n  resources: [],\n  summary: { totalResources: 0, totalOperations: 0, totalFields: 0 },\n};\n\nfor (const file of files) {\n  try {\n    const content = readFileSync(join(API_DIR, file), \"utf-8\");\n    const resource = parseApiPage(file, content);\n    if (resource) {\n      result.resources.push(resource);\n      result.totalParsed++;\n    }\n  } catch (err: any) {\n    result.parseFailures.push({ file, error: err.message });\n  }\n}\n\nresult.summary = {\n  totalResources: result.resources.length,\n 0),\n};\n\nwriteFileSync(OUTPUT, JSON.stringify(result, null, 2));\nconsole.log(\n  `Extracted ${result.resources.length} resources from ${result.totalFiles} files`\n);\nconsole.log(\n  `Total pages.\n *\n * Input: ../api-pages/*.html\n * Output: fhir-api-resources.json\n *\n * Run: bun run extract-fhir-api.ts\n */\n\nimport { readdirSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from \"fs\";\nimport { join } from \"path\";\n\nconst API_DIR = join(import.meta.dir, \"..\", \"api-pages\");\nconst OUTPUT = join(import.meta.dir, \"fhir-api-resources.json\");\n\ninterface FhirField {\n  name: string;\n  type: string;\n  required: boolean;\n  description: string;\n  valueOptions?: string[];\n  children?: FhirField[];\n}\n\ninterface FhirOperation {\n  method: string;\n  path: string;\n  summary: string;\n  attributes?: FhirField[];\n}\n\ninterface FhirResource {\n  name: string;\n  sourceFile: string;\n  sourceUrl: string;\n  lastUpdated: string | null;\n  description: string;\n  endpoints: string[];\n  operations: FhirOperation[];\n}\n\ninterface ExtractionResult {\n  extractionDate: string;\n  totalFiles: number;\n  totalParsed: number;\n  parseFailures: { file: string; error: string }[];\n  resources: FhirResource[];\n}\n\nfunction stripHtml(html: string): string {\n  return html\n    .replace(/<[^>]+>/g, \" \")\n    .replace(/&amp;/g, \"&\")\n    .replace(/&lt;/g, \"<\")\n    .replace(/&gt;/g, \">\")\n    .replace(/&quot;/g, '\"')\n    .replace(/&#39;/g, \"'\")\n    .replace(/&nbsp;/g, \" \")\n    .replace(/\\s+/g, \" \")\n    .trim();\n}\n\nfunction extractFieldsFromApidocObjects(html: string): FhirField[] {\n  const fields: FhirField[] = [];\n\n  // Match top-level apidoc__object blocks. These have nested structure.\n  // We parse from the flattened text representation instead of trying\n  // to do full DOM parsing. We'll use a regex approach to find\n  // name/type/required/description patterns.\n\n  // Find each apidoc__object (non-nested first pass)\n  const objectPattern =\n    /<div class=\"apidoc__object\">(.*?)(?=<div class=\"apidoc__object\">|<div class=\"apidoc__object__children__panel\">|$)/gs;\n\n  // Simpler approach: extract name+type+description sequences\n  const namePattern = /<div class=\"apidoc__object__name\">\\s*(.*?)\\s*<\\/div>/gs;\n  const typePattern = /<div class=\"apidoc__object__type\">\\s*(.*?)\\s*<\\/div>/gs;\n  const requiredPattern = /<div class=\"apidoc__object__required\">required<\\/div>/g;\n  const descPattern =\n    /<div class=\"apidoc__object__description\">\\s*(.*?)\\s*<\\/div>/gs;\n  const enumPattern =\n    /<div class=\"apidoc__object__enum_options\">\\s*(.*?)\\s*<\\/div>/gs;\n\n  // Get all names\n  const names: string[] = [];\n  let m;\n  while ((m = namePattern.exec(html)) !== null) {\n    names.push(stripHtml(m[1]));\n  }\n\n  const types: string[] = [];\n  while ((m = typePattern.exec(html)) !== null) {\n    types.push(stripHtml(m[1]));\n  }\n\n  const descriptions: string[] = [];\n  while ((m = descPattern.exec(html)) !== null) {\n    descriptions.push(stripHtml(m[1]));\n  }\n\n  // Build fields from aligned arrays\n  for (let i = 0; i < names.length; i++) {\n    const field: FhirField = {\n      name: names[i],\n      type: types[i] || \"\",\n      required: false, // We'll set this from context\n      description: descriptions[i] || \"\",\n    };\n    fields.push(field);\n  }\n\n  return fields;\n}\n\nfunction parseOperationBlock(\n  html: string,\n  method: string,\n  path: string,\n  summary: string\n): FhirOperation {\n  const fields = extractFieldsFromApidocObjects(html);\n  return {\n    method,\n    path,\n    summary,\n    attributes: fields.length > 0 ? fields : undefined,\n  };\n}\n\nfunction parseApiPage(\n  filename: string,\n  content: string\n): FhirResource | null {\n  // Extract resource name from title\n  const titleMatch = content.match(\n    /<h1 class=\"article__title\"[^>]*>(.*?)<\\/h1>/s\n  );\n  const resourceName = titleMatch ? stripHtml(titleMatch[1]) : filename.replace(\".html\", \"\");\n\n  // Skip non-resource pages\n  if (\n    [\n      \"introduction\",\n      \"service-base-urls\",\n      \"quickstart\",\n      \"authentication-best-practices\",\n      \"customer-authentication\",\n      \"conditional-requests\",\n      \"pagination\",\n      \"date-filtering\",\n      \"errors\",\n      \"software-requirements\",\n    ].includes(filename.replace(\".html\", \"\"))\n  ) {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  // Extract last updated date\n  const dateMatch = content.match(\n    /<time[^>]*>\\s*Last updated:\\s*(.*?)\\s*<\\/time>/\n  );\n  const lastUpdated = dateMatch ? dateMatch[1].trim() : null;\n\n  // Extract description (first paragraph or meta description)\n  const metaDescMatch = content.match(\n    /<meta name=\"description\" content=\"([^\"]*)\"/\n  );\n  const description = metaDescMatch ? metaDescMatch[1] : \"\";\n\n  // Extract endpoints\n  const endpointPattern =\n    /<div class=\"apidoc__endpoint__list__item\">.*?<span class=\"apidoc__endpoint__url\">(.*?)<\\/span>/gs;\n  const endpoints: string[] = [];\n  let em;\n  while ((em = endpointPattern.exec(content)) !== null) {\n    endpoints.push(stripHtml(em[1]));\n  }\n\n  // Extract operations - find sections by apidoc__info blocks\n  const operations: FhirOperation[] = [];\n\n  // Find operation info blocks\n  const infoPattern =\n    /<div class=\"apidoc__info\">\\s*<div class=\"apidoc__info__method[^\"]*\">(.*?)<\\/div>\\s*<span class=\"apidoc__info__url\">(.*?)<\\/span>\\s*<\\/div>\\s*<div class=\"apidoc__info__summary\">(.*?)<\\/div>/gs;\n\n  let im;\n  while ((im = infoPattern.exec(content)) !== null) {\n    const method = stripHtml(im[1]);\n    const path = stripHtml(im[2]);\n    const summary = stripHtml(im[3]);\n    operations.push({\n      method,\n      path,\n      summary,\n    });\n  }\n\n  // Now extract all fields from the entire apidoc section as the combined field set\n  const apidocMatch = content.match(/<div class=\"apidoc\">(.*)/s);\n  let allFields: FhirField[] = [];\n  if (apidocMatch) {\n    allFields = extractFieldsFromApidocObjects(apidocMatch[1]);\n  }\n\n  // Assign fields to operations based on position (create gets first batch, read gets second, etc.)\n  // For simplicity, we'll attach all fields to the resource level\n  if (operations.length > 0 && allFields.length > 0) {\n    operations[0].attributes = allFields;\n  }\n\n  return {\n    name: resourceName,\n    sourceFile: filename,\n    sourceUrl: `https://docs.canvasmedical.com/api/${filename.replace(\".html\", \"\")}/`,\n    lastUpdated,\n    description,\n    endpoints,\n    operations,\n  };\n}\n\n// Main\nconst files = readdirSync(API_DIR).filter((f) => f.endsWith(\".html\"));\nconst result: ExtractionResult = {\n  extractionDate: new Date().toISOString(),\n  totalFiles: files.length,\n  totalParsed: 0,\n  parseFailures: [],\n  resources: [],\n};\n\nfor (const file of files) {\n  try {\n    const content = readFileSync(join(API_DIR, file), \"utf-8\");\n    const resource = parseApiPage(file, content);\n    if (resource) {\n      result.resources.push(resource);\n      result.totalParsed++;\n    }\n  } catch (err: any) {\n    result.parseFailures.push({ file, error: err.message });\n  }\n}\n\nwriteFileSync(OUTPUT, JSON.stringify(result, null, 2));\nconsole.log(\n  `Extracted ${result.resources.length} resources from ${result.totalFiles} files`\n);\nconsole.log(`Parse failures: ${result.parseFailures.length}`);\nif (result.parseFailures.length > 0) {\n  for (const f of result.parseFailures) {\n    console.log(`  ${f.file}: ${f.error}`);\n  }\n}\n"}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01UD1zDkhFAHUeJ7GdpwH7BC","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_012wy3X4JzCrL7gLQN5aETCG","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/canvas-medical-inc--canvas-medical/ehi-export-report.md","content":"# Canvas Medical, Inc. — EHI Export Documentation\n\nCollected: 2026-02-16\n\n## Source\n- Registered URL: https://docs.canvasmedical.com/documentation/ehi-export/\n- CHPL IDs: 10904\n- CHPL Product Number: 15.04.04.3112.Canv.01.00.1.220523\n- Certification Date: 2022-05-23\n\n## Navigation Journal\n\n1. **Initial probe**: `curl -sI -L \"https://docs.canvasmedical.com/documentation/ehi-export/\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0'` returned HTTP 200 with `content-type: text/html`, 21,514 bytes, served from CloudFront/S3.\n\n2. **Page download**: `curl -sL \"https://docs.canvasmedical.com/documentation/ehi-export/\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o ehi-export.html` — clean static HTML page generated by Jekyll v4.2.1. Last updated 13 Nov 2025. No JavaScript required for core content.\n\n3. **Content extraction**: The EHI export page is a single HTML page describing the export mechanism. It lists 29 FHIR R4 resources included in the export, each linking to its FHIR API documentation page at `/api/{resource}/`.\n\n4. **FHIR API resource pages downloaded**: For each of the 29 resources listed on the EHI export page, downloaded the corresponding API documentation page:\n   ```bash\n   curl -sL \"https://docs.canvasmedical.com/api/{resource}\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o api-pages/{resource}.html\n   ```\n   All 29 pages returned HTTP 200 with rich field-level documentation using Canvas's custom `apidoc__object` HTML structure. Pages ranged from 85KB (Specimen) to 428KB (Patient).\n\n5. **SDK data model pages downloaded**: The Canvas SDK documentation at `/sdk/data-*` pages describes 51 internal data models (the platform's actual database/object model). These provide the ground truth for what data Canvas stores internally, independent of the FHIR export mapping. Downloaded all 52 pages (51 models + index):\n   ```bash\n   curl -sL \"https://docs.canvasmedical.com/sdk/data-{model}/\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o sdk-data-pages/data-{model}.html\n   ```\n\n6. **API Introduction and Service Base URLs**: Downloaded the FHIR API introduction page and the service base URLs page, plus the production FHIR endpoint listing (JSON):\n   ```bash\n   curl -sL \"https://docs.canvasmedical.com/assets/static/fhir-service-base-urls-production.json\" -o fhir-service-base-urls-production.json\n   ```\n   This lists 94 production FHIR endpoint base URLs, confirming active deployments.\n\n7. **FHIR metadata endpoint**: Attempted `curl -sI \"https://fumage.canvasmedical.com/metadata\"` — the fumage server requires a specific instance subdomain (e.g., `fumage-{instance}.canvasmedical.com`), so no public CapabilityStatement is accessible without an instance name.\n\n8. **Screenshot**: Captured full-page screenshot of the EHI export documentation page.\n\n9. **Enrichment**: Built two Bun TypeScript extraction scripts that parse all downloaded HTML into structured JSON:\n   - `extract-fhir-api.ts` → 29 resources, 84 operations, 3,698 fields\n   - `extract-sdk-models.ts` → 51 data model pages, 136 models, 1,811 fields, 57 enums\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### Export Mechanism\n\nCanvas's EHI export is built on top of **FHIR Bulk Data Access** (STU 1.0.1). The export produces **FHIR R4 resources in Newline Delimited JSON (NDJSON)** format.\n\nTwo export paths exist:\n\n1. **Single patient export via UI**: Navigate to \"Fhir bulk data exports\" in admin settings, click \"ADD FHIR BULK DATA EXPORT\", select an OAuth application, search for a patient, and click \"Start Export.\" This generates downloadable NDJSON files for each resource type. Access is permission-based.\n\n2. **Single patient export via API**: `GET https://fumage-{instance}.canvasmedical.com/Patient/{id}/$export`\n\n3. **Population export**: Contact Canvas support with instance name, username, role, patient group, and purpose. Canvas staff initiates the bulk export on behalf of the customer. Output appears in the same \"FHIR Bulk Data Export\" admin table.\n\n### Resources in Scope\n\nThe EHI export includes **29 FHIR R4 resource types** — explicitly described as \"the patient resource itself\" plus \"any resource that is associated with the patient\":\n\n| Resource | Endpoints | Fields Documented |\n|---|---|---|\n| AllergyIntolerance | POST, GET, PUT, GET(search) | 196 |\n| Appointment | POST, GET, PUT, GET(search) | 206 |\n| CarePlan | GET, GET(search) | 50 |\n| CareTeam | GET, PUT, GET(search) | 85 |\n| Claim | POST, GET, PUT, GET(search) | 292 |\n| Communication | POST, GET, GET(search) | 71 |\n| Condition | POST, GET, PUT, GET(search) | 176 |\n| Consent | POST, GET, GET(search) | 69 |\n| Coverage | POST, GET, PUT, GET(search) | 209 |\n| CoverageEligibilityResponse | GET, GET(search) | 108 |\n| Device | GET, GET(search) | 61 |\n| DiagnosticReport | GET, GET(search) | 94 |\n| DocumentReference | POST, GET, GET(search) | 163 |\n| Encounter | GET, GET(search) | 138 |\n| Goal | GET, GET(search) | 79 |\n| Immunization | POST, GET, PUT, GET(search) | 123 |\n| Media | POST, GET, GET(search) | 87 |\n| MedicationDispense | GET, GET(search) | 81 |\n| MedicationRequest | GET, GET(search) | 126 |\n| MedicationStatement | POST, GET, PUT, GET(search) | 124 |\n| Observation | POST, GET, GET(search) | 210 |\n| Patient | POST, GET, PUT, GET(search) | 332 |\n| Procedure | GET, GET(search) | 49 |\n| Provenance | GET, GET(search) | 77 |\n| QuestionnaireResponse | POST, GET, PUT, GET(search) | 135 |\n| RelatedPerson | GET, GET(search) | 83 |\n| ServiceRequest | GET, GET(search) | 77 |\n| Specimen | GET, GET(search) | 33 |\n| Task | POST, GET, PUT, GET(search) | 164 |\n\n### Documentation Depth\n\nEach FHIR resource page provides:\n- Full field-level attribute documentation (name, type, required/optional, description)\n- Value set options for coded fields\n- Nested child attribute documentation\n- Canvas-specific extensions (e.g., note-id extension, claim queue extension)\n- HTTP response codes and error documentation\n\nThe FHIR API documentation uses Canvas-specific profiles — these aren't just standard FHIR R4. For example, AllergyIntolerance references US Core STU3.1.1 profile but includes Canvas-specific extensions for note linkage. Claim includes custom queue extensions. Patient includes Canvas-specific extensions for social determinants (housing, education, veteran status, etc.).\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nCanvas's internal data model (as documented in the SDK) has **51 data model pages** describing **136 models with 1,811 fields**. The EHI export covers **29 FHIR R4 resource types** — a significant portion but not everything.\n\n**Clearly covered domains:**\n- **Demographics**: Patient, RelatedPerson — comprehensive coverage including Canvas-specific extensions for race, ethnicity, sex-at-birth, gender identity, social determinants\n- **Clinical problems & conditions**: Condition — full CRUD with clinical/verification status\n- **Allergies**: AllergyIntolerance — including reaction severity and manifestation\n- **Medications**: MedicationRequest, MedicationStatement, MedicationDispense — covers prescriptions, active medications, and dispensing\n- **Immunizations**: Immunization — full coverage\n- **Lab results**: DiagnosticReport, Observation, Specimen — covers lab results and vitals\n- **Clinical notes & encounters**: Encounter, DocumentReference — encounters map to Canvas notes\n- **Procedures**: Procedure — basic coverage\n- **Care plans & goals**: CarePlan, Goal — included\n- **Care teams**: CareTeam — included\n- **Devices**: Device — implantable device tracking\n- **Billing/Claims**: Claim, Coverage, CoverageEligibilityResponse — Canvas includes billing data in the export, which is notably better than many vendors\n- **Communication**: Communication — maps to Canvas messages\n- **Consent**: Consent — patient consent records\n- **Questionnaires**: QuestionnaireResponse — patient form responses\n- **Media**: Media — includes images and attachments\n- **Tasks**: Task — includes task management\n- **Provenance**: Provenance — data origin tracking\n- **Referrals/Orders**: ServiceRequest — covers referrals and service requests\n\n**Domains with potential gaps (internal models without clear FHIR export mapping):**\n- **Assessment**: Canvas stores assessments as a distinct data model (18 fields including narrative, ICD-10 codes, background, status). These may be partially captured in Encounter or Condition resources, but the assessment model's narrative structure may lose fidelity.\n- **BillingLineItem**: While Claim is in the export, Canvas internally stores billing line items as distinct objects with CPT codes, charges, units, and modifiers linked to notes. The Claim resource may not capture all billing line item detail.\n- **ChargeDescriptionMaster / PayorSpecificCharge**: The CDM and payor-specific charge tables are internal billing configuration. While individual patient billing is in Claims, the charge catalog itself is not exported.\n- **CompoundMedication**: Canvas tracks compound medications separately. These may or may not be represented in MedicationRequest/MedicationStatement.\n- **Letter**: Canvas has a distinct Letter model (8 fields) for patient correspondence. It's unclear whether letters are captured in Communication or DocumentReference.\n- **Posting**: Canvas has 10 posting-related models (78 fields) for payment posting, claim payments, adjustments, etc. This detailed revenue cycle data does not appear to have a FHIR resource in the export.\n- **Referral**: Canvas has a detailed referral model (53 fields across 3 sub-models) that may be partially represented by ServiceRequest but likely loses internal referral tracking detail.\n- **ReasonForVisit**: Distinct from encounter diagnoses. May be captured in Encounter.reason but unclear.\n- **Stop Medication Event**: Distinct events for medication discontinuation. May be partially in MedicationStatement/MedicationRequest.\n\n**Domains that are NOT EHI (correctly excluded from export):**\n- **Application, Calendar, CanvasUser, Staff, BusinessLine, Facility, Organization, PracticeLocation, ServiceProvider, Team**: These are system configuration and provider-facing records, not patient-facing clinical data.\n- **ProtocolCurrent, ProtocolOverride**: Clinical decision support configuration.\n- **Command**: Internal charting UI construct.\n- **CommonEnumerationType, ValueSets**: Reference data / system configuration.\n- **ExternalEvent**: ADT feed events — operational/integration data.\n- **LabPartner & LabPartnerTest**: Lab partner configuration, not patient results.\n- **BannerAlert**: While these are patient-facing alerts, they represent transient UI state rather than permanent clinical records. Borderline — could be argued either way.\n- **DetectedIssue**: Listed in the FHIR API docs (has CRUD endpoints) but NOT listed in the EHI export resource table. This is a gap — DetectedIssues (drug interactions, contraindications) are clinically relevant patient data.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export format is **FHIR R4 NDJSON via Bulk Data Access** — a well-established standard. This is a strong format choice because:\n- It's machine-readable and well-structured\n- FHIR R4 provides rich semantics for healthcare data\n- Bulk Data Access is an established pattern for large exports\n- NDJSON enables streaming/line-by-line processing\n\nCanvas uses **US Core STU3.1.1** profiles as a baseline for most resources (based on profile references in the API documentation) but extends them with Canvas-specific extensions. This provides reasonable interoperability while accommodating Canvas-specific data.\n\nHowever, this is fundamentally the **same FHIR API that backs Canvas's (g)(10) certification** for standardized patient access. The question is whether the 29 resources listed for the EHI export represent a broader set than what (g)(10) requires, or whether they're the same set. Comparing to US Core:\n\n**Beyond US Core (additional resources in Canvas EHI export):**\n- Claim — billing data, not in US Core\n- Communication — not in US Core STU3.1.1\n- Consent — not in standard US Core\n- Coverage — insurance data, not in standard US Core\n- CoverageEligibilityResponse — not in US Core\n- Media — not in standard US Core\n- QuestionnaireResponse — not in US Core STU3.1.1\n- RelatedPerson — not in standard US Core\n- ServiceRequest — not in US Core STU3.1.1\n- Specimen — not in US Core STU3.1.1\n- Task — not in US Core STU3.1.1\n\nThis is encouraging — Canvas has included ~11 resource types beyond what US Core / (g)(10) would require, particularly billing (Claim, Coverage, CoverageEligibilityResponse) and operational data (Task, Communication). This suggests Canvas has given genuine thought to (b)(10) scope rather than simply repackaging their (g)(10) API.\n\nThat said, some internal data domains (detailed payment postings, compound medications, assessments) don't have clear FHIR R4 resource mappings and may be lost or compressed in the export.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The EHI export page is clear and straightforward — it explains the mechanism, lists the resources, and links to detailed API docs.\n- Each of the 29 FHIR resource API pages has rich field-level documentation with types, descriptions, required markers, value options, and Canvas-specific extensions.\n- The SDK documentation provides an unusually transparent view of Canvas's internal data model, making coverage assessment possible.\n- Total documented fields across all FHIR resources: **3,698 fields across 84 operations**.\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- No sample export files are provided — there's no example of what an actual NDJSON output looks like for a patient.\n- No data dictionary mapping between Canvas internal models and FHIR resources — one has to infer how internal data maps to FHIR output.\n- No documentation of what happens to data that doesn't fit into FHIR R4 — do Canvas-specific extensions preserve all internal fields?\n- The population export requires contacting support, which adds friction for legitimate EHI export requests.\n- No explicit (b)(10) framing — the page doesn't mention the regulatory basis or explain how the 29 resources were chosen to satisfy the \"all EHI\" requirement.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Field-level granularity**: Excellent. Every resource has field-by-field documentation including:\n- Field name and data type\n- Required/optional markers\n- Text descriptions\n- Supported value sets for coded fields\n- Canvas-specific extensions with their URLs and value types\n- Nested object structures\n\n**Coded fields**: Well documented with supported value sets (e.g., claim status codes, clinical status codes, verification status codes).\n\n**Relationships**: Documented via FHIR references (e.g., `Patient/id`, `Encounter/id`). Internal Canvas relationships (e.g., note → billing line items) are documented in the SDK but not explicitly in the FHIR export docs.\n\n**Versioning**: Pages show \"Last updated\" dates (ranging from 2024 to 2025). No formal changelog or version history.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://docs.canvasmedical.com/documentation/ehi-export/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (main page) + one_click (each resource API page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n- **FHIR metadata endpoint**: The fumage FHIR server requires a specific instance subdomain, so no public CapabilityStatement is accessible for verification.\n- **Population export gated by support**: Population-level EHI export (all patients or groups) requires contacting Canvas support rather than being available as a self-service operation. This is a procedural barrier noted in the documentation.\n- **No downloadable schema files**: No OpenAPI spec, JSON Schema, or machine-readable profile definitions are provided alongside the documentation. The HTML API documentation is the primary artifact.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1783,"cache_read_input_tokens":93521,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1783},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9b94a45a-b6b8-4ed5-bf4c-a48de7a8d22e","uuid":"4c4e4d56-8f19-4377-b9f2-323c6689956f"}

Comparing to US Core:\n\n**Beyond US Core (additional resources in Canvas EHI export):**\n- Claim — billing data, not in US Core\n- Communication — not in US Core STU3.1.1\n- Consent — not in standard US Core\n- Coverage — insurance data, not in standard US Core\n- CoverageEligibilityResponse — not in US Core\n- Media — not in standard US Core\n- QuestionnaireResponse — not in US Core STU3.1.1\n- RelatedPerson — not in standard US Core\n- ServiceRequest — not in US Core STU3.1.1\n- Specimen — not in US Core STU3.1.1\n- Task — not in US Core STU3.1.1\n\nThis is encouraging — Canvas has included ~11 resource types beyond what US Core / (g)(10) would require, particularly billing (Claim, Coverage, CoverageEligibilityResponse) and operational data (Task, Communication). This suggests Canvas has given genuine thought to (b)(10) scope rather than simply repackaging their (g)(10) API.\n\nThat said, some internal data domains (detailed payment postings, compound medications, assessments) don't have clear FHIR R4 resource mappings and may be lost or compressed in the export.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The EHI export page is clear and straightforward — it explains the mechanism, lists the resources, and links to detailed API docs.\n- Each of the 29 FHIR resource API pages has rich field-level documentation with types, descriptions, required markers, value options, and Canvas-specific extensions.\n- The SDK documentation provides an unusually transparent view of Canvas's internal data model, making coverage assessment possible.\n- Total documented fields across all FHIR resources: **3,698 fields across 84 operations**.\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- No sample export files are provided — there's no example of what an actual NDJSON output looks like for a patient.\n- No data dictionary mapping between Canvas internal models and FHIR resources — one has to infer how internal data maps to FHIR output.\n- No documentation of what happens to data that doesn't fit into FHIR R4 — do Canvas-specific extensions preserve all internal fields?\n- The population export requires contacting support, which adds friction for legitimate EHI export requests.\n- No explicit (b)(10) framing — the page doesn't mention the regulatory basis or explain how the 29 resources were chosen to satisfy the \"all EHI\" requirement.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Field-level granularity**: Excellent. Every resource has field-by-field documentation including:\n- Field name and data type\n- Required/optional markers\n- Text descriptions\n- Supported value sets for coded fields\n- Canvas-specific extensions with their URLs and value types\n- Nested object structures\n\n**Coded fields**: Well documented with supported value sets (e.g., claim status codes, clinical status codes, verification status codes).\n\n**Relationships**: Documented via FHIR references (e.g., `Patient/id`, `Encounter/id`). Internal Canvas relationships (e.g., note → billing line items) are documented in the SDK but not explicitly in the FHIR export docs.\n\n**Versioning**: Pages show \"Last updated\" dates (ranging from 2024 to 2025). No formal changelog or version history.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://docs.canvasmedical.com/documentation/ehi-export/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (main page) + one_click (each resource API page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n- **FHIR metadata endpoint**: The fumage FHIR server requires a specific instance subdomain, so no public CapabilityStatement is accessible for verification.\n- **Population export gated by support**: Population-level EHI export (all patients or groups) requires contacting Canvas support rather than being available as a self-service operation. This is a procedural barrier noted in the documentation.\n- **No downloadable schema files**: No OpenAPI spec, JSON Schema, or machine-readable profile definitions are provided alongside the documentation.
